**Rejected alternatives:** Inventing schema fields, returning normally from a faulted R call or labelling a fixed-database subdomain full-D proof misstates ISA 0.9. Evidence still supports the rung's declared-space test; no rung is removed.

**Conformance consequence:** DM/DD/DX: exact args bits/token tuples and budgets, wrong expected relation/invocation negative, fixed DB rows × all target/fault values with coverage label and zero fault; distinguish native-comparable/reference fault/layout-dependent/classifier-undefined outcomes.

**Dependency order:** I05/I06/I08/I14/I-bz6; D01 for schedule-bound claims. Track unresolved wording here, not a new duplicate ISA capability bead.

**Maintainer choice:** D22 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

## Part C — 0.3 item records

<a id="i02"></a>
### I02 — Effective ctrl and R budgets

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.2`. **Group:** 0.3. **Decision:** D18. **Audit:** 1.4-e, D18, B-43.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 made ctrl/reversible budgets inert.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-9.3-P2, DLT-9.3-P3, DLT-10-P37 and DLT-17.6 already enforce generation/slice/R budgets; DLT-17.6-P2 and DLT-25.1-P4 still classify every R entry as BLK-5.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** K5/K6: ISA-4.1-P14, ISA-4.9-P13 and ISA-10 provide typed terminal faults and eligible failed-run reversal; they do not supply the dialect instruction counter.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> R budget exhaustion MUST lower the existing pre-instruction counter check to fault 1, 2 or 3, preserve the preceding history and execute no later source effect. [deps: DLT-17.6-P1, DLT-17.6-P2, DLT-10-P37, ISA-4.1-P14, ISA-4.9-P13, ISA-10]

**Rejected alternatives:** An inert budget, interpreter host fuel, post-effect checks and generic trap substituted for fault C all change observable outcomes.

**Conformance consequence:** DE/DS: r-budget-prefix and n-rstep-return become executed ISA-0.9 success/fault/reverse cases; DT keeps full independent slice budgets and nested generation accounting.

**Dependency order:** I05, I33, I-bz6; I20 for device-computed fields.

**Maintainer choice:** D18 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i19"></a>
### I19 — Runtime register widths

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.19`. **Group:** 0.3. **Decision:** D15. **Audit:** B-07 / ISA-4.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 rejected n not finitely enumerated; the capability is real dynamic allocation and handle widths, not only a build-time SQLite N.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-3.4, DLT-6.5-P14 and DLT-25.1-P2 still use BLK-1 for top classical n; DLT-10-P42 also blocks a Co width with top value set.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** K1: ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-3.3-P8, ISA-3.3-P10, ISA-3.3-P12, ISA-3.3-P19 supplies qreg[*], dynamic new/slice/width and fixed-transfer checks. It does not permit a quantum location as a classical allocation operand; Co allocation still needs unconditional padding.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Classical runtime widths MUST lower through qreg[*] with the existing n>=1 dialect check and checked transfers, and a coherent width MUST allocate unconditionally to a sound bound derived from materialised instructions or, absent a tighter bound, the full unsigned marker-width domain, with out-of-range wire operations predicated off. [deps: DLT-3.4, DLT-10-P42, DLT-18.3, ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-3.3-P8, ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-3.5]

Additional proposed width/shape sentences, confined to the 0.3 track:

> When an interface width or descriptor extent is not finitely enumerated by AV, the reference dispatch domain MUST include every valid representable shape allowed by the marker's unsigned i64 fields and the existing parameter-binding checks, rather than treating AV top as a runtime interface failure. [deps: DLT-3.2, DLT-8.1, DLT-8.2, DLT-8.4, ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-3.3-P19]

> Each such branch MUST retain the fixed-signature reference-object map and its exact fault and budget semantics, with width selection classical and with no inspection of coherent data to select a shape. [deps: DLT-8.4, DLT-16.2, DLT-9.4, ISA-3.1, ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-5.2]

Design consequence: the valid unsigned-i64 width/extent domain is finite even when the AV representation widens to top, so exhaustive static shape dispatch is a conservative construction, not a claim of efficient compilation. For a Co width, the padding bound is classical and fixed before entry; comparisons against the logical width remain coherent predicates. A more compact lowering is acceptable only with the same semantics; JIT object admission or a parametric region-signature extension is not assumed here. Invalid values and actual resource exhaustion retain the existing named outcomes. This replaces the obsolete top-width blocker case in 0.3; it does not silently transfer it to T-IFACE.

**Rejected alternatives:** Keeping the 4096-atom cap as a capability bound, substituting a user constant, or inspecting a superposed width classically are narrowings/errors. K1 does not by itself justify deleting the Co case without a construction.

**Conformance consequence:** DB/DS: launch- and measurement-derived n, zero/overflow/size mismatch before mutation, widths beyond finite analysis cap, dynamic slice/index; DT a small Co width padded to a known bound plus top-domain construction check. DO extends shape enumeration over valid representable widths/sizes without a catch-all T-IFACE for a valid shape.

**Dependency order:** I17, I33, I25; K1 publication/implementation in D01. The type-domain fallback can be extremely costly; staging is mechanics only.

**Maintainer choice:** D15 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i20"></a>
### I20 — Device-computed budgets

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.20`. **Group:** 0.3. **Decision:** D16. **Audit:** B-08 / ISA-1.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 rejected runtime budgets; the accepted version supports static and launch-supplied fields but not arbitrary device computations.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-9.5-P2, DLT-9.5-P3 already mint/select host tokens; DLT-9.5-P4 and DLT-25.1-P3 still issue BLK-2 for device-derived fields.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** K3: ISA-4.1-P17, ISA-5.4-P3 and ISA-7.4-P13 mint validated run-scoped tokens, revoke them on reversal and bind schedule/args to exact tuples.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> A device-computed classical budget tuple MUST be resolved with the existing field precedence and minted by budget.mint before region entry, without rounding or substituting any component. [deps: DLT-9.1-P7, DLT-9.2-P8, DLT-9.5-P4, DLT-9.5-P5, ISA-4.1-P17, ISA-5.4-P3, ISA-7.4-P13]

**Rejected alternatives:** Finite-token rounding changes F_B; minting inside a coherent region or using another invocation's schedule violates ISA binding.

**Conformance consequence:** DD/DM/DS: measured-64-bit budget witness becomes accept/executed; invalid tuple causes code4 before mutation, revocation/use-after-close negative, schedule B=3 not reused for B=2 and same tuple/different token ids hash equally.

**Dependency order:** I05/I33, I-bz6 for typed mint failures, D01; lproc token forwarding follows Q14 unchanged.

**Maintainer choice:** D16 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i25"></a>
### I25 — Handles stored through loaded/returned pointers

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.25`. **Group:** 0.3. **Decision:** D17. **Audit:** B-24.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 excluded Vec/std::vector buffers whose pointers were loaded from collection headers or returned by functions.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-6.4-P1 already admits tracked slots through DLT-5.2; DLT-6.5-P10/25.1-P2 still block memory-reaching sites with infinite reference instance bounds.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** K1 supplies ISA-3.3-P13, ISA-3.3-P14 id/from_id plus width/state checks and ownership through CLOSED; K2 covers their use in R. This removes static-register-file naming as a capability limit.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Memory-reaching handles with no finite reference instance bound MUST use a persistent lossless tag-to-ledger-id mapping and qreg.from_id at use, preserving pointer-width tags, identity, liveness, ownership and checked widths. [deps: DLT-6.1-P1, DLT-6.4-P1, DLT-6.5-P10, ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-3.3-P13, ISA-3.3-P14, ISA-3.3-P19, ISA-10]

**Rejected alternatives:** Keeping the 64-slot fallback as a 0.3 limit or narrowing every tag to a truncated 64-bit launch id reintroduces bounds/aliasing; converting ids does not permit arithmetic on source handles.

**Conformance consequence:** DB/DS: grow Vec/list beyond 64 simultaneously live registers, loaded/returned buffer pointers, stale/viewed/foreign-run id negatives and pointer-width bridge; existing bounded/direct cases stay semantically equal.

**Dependency order:** I11/I22/I35, I19, I-cbe; shares one implementation/reference design with I29.

**Maintainer choice:** D17 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i29"></a>
### I29 — Handles across device entries

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.29`. **Group:** 0.3. **Decision:** D17. **Audit:** B-30.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 forced release at each device-entry return, excluding REPL lexical lifetimes.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-6.6-P1 already uses QREG parameters/results and program-end leak checking; DLT-6.5-P7 still blocks persistent memory roots without finite bounds.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** K1: ISA-3.3-P14, ISA-3.3-P20, ISA-3.3-P21 and ISA-10 distinguish identity/liveness from exclusive open-run ownership; aliases are not independent registers.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> QREG parameters and results MUST retain their register identity across entries and may outlive a device-entry return, with persistent lookup subject to the ISA run-ownership and CLOSED rules and leak checking at program end. [deps: DLT-6.5-P7, DLT-6.6-P1, ISA-3.3-P14, ISA-3.3-P20, ISA-3.3-P21, ISA-10]

**Rejected alternatives:** Entry-local release breaks lexical lifetime; simultaneous access by separate open runs or treating aliases as disjoint violates the ledger.

**Conformance consequence:** DB/DD/DS: repeated entries retaining more than 64 roots, parameter-to-global-to-later-entry, QREG return, ownership refusal while original run remains open, and valid reuse after close.

**Dependency order:** I25/I-cbe/I19; no new source marker signature.

**Maintainer choice:** D17 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i32"></a>
### I32 — Quantum markers inside R closures

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.32`. **Group:** 0.3. **Decision:** D18. **Audit:** B-38.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 banned all markers in reversible closures, although only measurement is intrinsically disallowed for reversal.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-7.5 already supports gate/region calls on env handles; DLT-25.1-P5 still blocks unbounded R allocation and R entry-state registers (BLK-4).

**ISA 0.9 provision:** K2: ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-3.3-P18 and ISA-4.1-P15 allow qreg operations in R and reverse exact ledger frames; measurement stays CC-only.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> R allocations, releases, slices and concatenations MUST use the ISA ledger-frame semantics, restoring original ids and alias states on reversal without a static hoisting-instance bound. [deps: DLT-7.5, DLT-25.1-P5, ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-3.3-P18, ISA-4.1-P15, ISA-7.4]

**Rejected alternatives:** A permanent hoisting cap narrows dynamic allocation; replacing reversed free with a fresh unrelated id breaks aliases; allowing R measurement breaks reverse eligibility.

**Conformance consequence:** DB/DE/DS: R allocate/use/free beyond finite bound, uncall/whole-run reverse through concat/slice and retained aliases, unverified-free refusal on non-verifying executor; measurement negative retained.

**Dependency order:** I19/I25 and I-bz6, K2/K6 implementation; unlocks I40 R snapshot.

**Maintainer choice:** D18 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i38"></a>
### I38 — Whole-program reversible mode

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.38`. **Group:** 0.3. **Decision:** D18. **Audit:** O-04.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 had no marker-free entry route for unmodified programs.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-19.2 already synthesises a CC launch stub and R entry, argv/envp, exit status, device tape and reversal, but DLT-11.5-P2 applies BLK-5 to every R entry.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-10, ISA-7.3 and ISA-7.4 and K5/K6 cover whole-run completed/failed reversal with explicit eligibility; faulting R calls do not return a recoverable status to CC.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Whole-program R mode MUST retain the unmodified entry route and effect tape, and MUST report terminal faults and reverse eligible completed or failed runs according to ISA 0.9. [deps: DLT-19.2, DLT-14.7, DLT-18.6, ISA-4.9-P1, ISA-7.4, ISA-10]

**Rejected alternatives:** Adding source markers to musl/kernel main narrows unmodified execution; executing a CC successor after R failure contradicts K5.

**Conformance consequence:** DD/DE/DS: argv/env/status, puts tape forward/reverse/replay, failed run and open activation reversal; DX3/5/6 actual programs remain gates beyond the minis.

**Dependency order:** I-bz6, I13/I36/I37, I32 when program allocates registers; D01 implementation gate.

**Maintainer choice:** D18 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i40"></a>
### I40 — Runtime entry-state snapshots

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.40`. **Group:** 0.3. **Decision:** D19. **Audit:** O-07.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 omitted transfer of caller-mutated globals/heap and allocator metadata into an oracle, including a post-boot snapshot.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-8.5 and DLT-16.2-P20 already specify replay with sizes/liveness/bump, including allocation-only closures; DLT-8.5-P7 still needs BLK-4 when R prepares the snapshot.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-7.1 and ISA-7.2 plus qreg concat and calls already realise replay/copy; K2 now permits preparation/release in R. G-12 overlay is absent but not needed for capability.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Runtime oracle entry state MUST retain caller bytes and allocator replay in native address order before any wrapper-private allocation, and R callers MUST use reversible register preparation and release for that same snapshot construction. [deps: DLT-8.5, DLT-16.2-P20, ISA-7.1, ISA-7.2, ISA-3.3-P18, ISA-7.4]

**Rejected alternatives:** Link-time images alone omit runtime state; resetting an allocation-only closure's allocator changes addresses; zeroing replayed inputs or silently requiring a small snapshot narrows the kernel path.

**Conformance consequence:** DO/DM/DD/DS: entry-replay-byte, entry-allocation-only and driver-snapshot-initializer; add R snapshot + reverse through metadata changes and stale cache invalidation on alloc/free/stack restore. DX7 compares the same runtime snapshot per basis input.

**Dependency order:** I33/I17/I32/I-bz6; fixed build snapshot remains a compatible path, G-12 is efficiency only.

**Maintainer choice:** D19 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

## Part C — ISA-blocked item records

No genuinely missing ISA 0.9 capability sentence was found for these items. The exact formerly missing sentences are cited below; neither a duplicate proposal bead nor an assertion of implemented semantics follows from draft acceptance. K64/K65 are the separate scheduling ratification gate in D01.

<a id="i-bz6"></a>
### I-bz6 — Typed R faults and reversible prefixes

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-bz6`. **Group:** ISA-blocked. **Decision:** D18. **Audit:** ISA-7 / BLK-5.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 generic R traps could not promise an exact cause; every promised named fault and intact prefix is required, including budget boundaries.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-11.5-P1, DLT-11.5-P2, DLT-11.5-P3, DLT-17.6-P5 and DLT-25.1-P4 explicitly preserve the missing contract and classify R entries blocked on ISA 0.8.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** Exact required ISA sentence is already present in ISA-4.1-P14.S1: fault C records exact nonzero C (zero becomes4), ends FAILED, runs no successor and retains prefix history; ISA-4.9-P12, ISA-4.9-P13 and ISA-10 complete reporting/reversal. No new ISA proposal.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> On the ISA 0.9 target, an R entry MUST report the exact effective fault cause through the launch result, preserve its entire logical prefix and stop without returning results or running its CC caller's successor. [deps: DLT-11.5-P3, DLT-17.6-P5, ISA-4.1-P14, ISA-4.9-P1, ISA-4.9-P12, ISA-4.9-P13, ISA-10]

**Rejected alternatives:** Generic trap, CC-only hostcall from R, or an artificial return-status recovery path changes the contract. Draft ISA wording is not executed evidence.

**Conformance consequence:** DE/DS blocker→accept on 0.9: r-fault-code (2), r-budget-prefix (1), implicit causes5/6/7, program-defined code above10, fault0→4, one record, zero later effect, reverse removes record and restores prefix/open activations. Keep old0.8 blocker vectors in their versioned corpus.

**Dependency order:** D01 ISA publication and independent interpreter support; then I02/I32/I38/I40. Status remains open.

**Maintainer choice:** D18 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i-cbe"></a>
### I-cbe — Cross-entry root accumulation

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-cbe`. **Group:** ISA-blocked. **Decision:** D17. **Audit:** ISA-4 / ISA-12.

**0.1 capability/history:** The original entry-local model omitted an unbounded sequence of live host-retained QREG parameters/results, while the north star requires REPL lifetimes.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-6.5-P6, DLT-6.5-P7 and DLT-25.1-P2, DLT-25.1-P5 correctly use infinity and BLK-1/BLK-4 rather than claiming N=1; the bounded 64-slot fallback is not the promised general solution.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** Exact needed sentences already exist: ISA-3.3-P14.S1 gives live id lookup and run ownership; P7/P8 give runtime widths; P18 reverses R ledger operations and resurrects the same id. K1/K2 therefore cover the semantic dependency; no new ISA bead.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Persistent parameter and result roots MUST resolve through the program ledger across entry calls without an entry-count or static-file bound, while retaining the ISA's exclusive open-run ownership and same-id reversal rules. [deps: DLT-6.5-P6, DLT-6.5-P7, DLT-6.6, ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-3.3-P14, ISA-3.3-P18, ISA-10]

**Rejected alternatives:** Per-entry trip counts do not bound lifetime; applying a blanket 64-slot limit or reusing stale tags narrows/corrupts the accepted lifetime contract.

**Conformance consequence:** DB/DD/DS blocker→accept on 0.9: handle-cross-entry plus parameter/result/global variants beyond64, active ancestor setjmp, caller-guaranteed free, exact-root free and R reversal. Check held-open ownership error separately from valid sequential closed runs.

**Dependency order:** D01 K1/K2 implementation/publication, I19/I25/I29; I32 supplies R allocation. Status remains open.

**Maintainer choice:** D17 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

## Part D. Dependency order and conformance gates

The editing dependency graph, expressed as landing waves (items within a wave can share one implementation change; this is a design order, not delegation):

1. **D01 baseline/version ledger:** pin source digests and clause IDs, inventory every retained rule/example, distinguish0.8 and0.9 expectations; complete0.2.11 clarifications independently of ISA adoption.
2. **Analysis and shared identity foundation:** I11/I22/I27/I35 plus PT12 I-0y2; keep S2 as one fixpoint with candidate/provenance/value flows. I-0t9 is independent regression-only bookkeeping.
3. **Budget/interface foundation:** I33 then I05; I17/I06 and existing I40 replay; I07/I15/I37/I12/I36 map the complete closure in the accepted normalisation order.
4. **ISA 0.9 runtime foundation:** I-bz6 (typed terminal cause and reversal), I19 (classical widths and coherent padding), I20 (minting), I25/I29/I-cbe (persistent lookup/ownership), then I32 (R ledger allocations) and I40 (R snapshots). These are linked capabilities, not selectable scope cuts.
5. **Composition:** I01/I31 TDL with I02 budgets, I03/I14 in-place alternatives, I08 channels, I13 models; I38 whole-program R and I39 platform; remaining collection/attribute/driver rows reuse these foundations.
6. **Freeze and bead closure evidence:** full versioned spec/checker/corpus gates, actual rung programs and independent contracts; no automatic closure from outline ratification.

Proposed gate sentences:

- Each new or changed rule MUST have a positive checked example, each retained rejection a negative, and each genuine blocker its version-specific blocked example, with independent execution rather than producer self-comparison. [deps: DLT-1.5, DLT-21.3, DLT-21.4, ISA-15]
- An unimplemented required example MUST fail the gate without being skipped, deleted or reclassified to fit the checker. [deps: DLT-1.5, DLT-21.4]
- An ISA 0.9 capability formerly blocked on 0.8 MUST have executed state, fault and reversal evidence before its bead is closed, and a passing legacy blocked-verdict test MUST NOT stand in for that execution. [deps: DLT-1.6, DLT-18.7, DLT-25.1, ISA-15]
- Scheduled0.9 objects and realised-operation claims MUST use the final ratified support/cleanup ordinal rules and bound reference expansion library before being reported conformant. [deps: ISA-11, ISA-11.1, ISA-12.1, ISA-12.3]

Baseline receipt is **2767/2767**,84 checker units and7 legacy tests at0.2.10 from its PROGRESS. This outline does not re-run or claim a0.9 freeze. The new table adds obligations; it does not lower baseline expectations or imply all listed new cases already exist. DA–DX/DL are the existing suite names in DLT-21.3; row names above identify concrete existing vectors or explicitly described new cases. Changes to checker code, examples, manifests and accepted files belong to the later draft/implementation job after rulings.

## Part E. Demo-ladder scope check

| NS rung | Retained full programme | Critical item records | Acceptance consequence |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | C/Rust/Julia scalar functions, including remaining calls and multi-argument functions | I06/I07/I12/I17/I35 | Exhaustive Int8 full declared D; both executors and materialised native comparison, zero fault |
| 2 | Pointer/struct/byte-memory hashtable | I07/I11/I17/I33/I40 | Native result, byte-exact reversal, heap/stack/depth boundaries |
| 3 | Unmodified musl hello-world | I13/I15/I36/I37/I38/I-bz6 | Console equal to native, un-run to reset, deterministic tape replay |
| 4 | **Grover over unmodified SQLite, core deliverable** | I05/I06/I07/I13/I15/I17/I22/I33/I35/I37 | Real SQL rowid query over fixed database in quantum-addressed VFS; all rows/all targets atN<=2^10, zero fault; Grover branch enumerationN<=2^8; estimateN=2^20 with budget/padded cost and named target |
| 5 | Julia push!/Dict on shim runtime | I11/I12/I16/I35/I38/I39 | Allocation-driven behaviour, native agreement and exact reversal; no compiler special cases for containers |
| 6 | xv6 then Linux tinyconfig | I13/I32/I35/I36/I37/I38/I39 | Same-image QEMU output, un-boot to reset vector, byte-identical tape; full platform inventory remains required mechanics |
| 7 | Kernel as oracle on syscall arguments and a post-boot snapshot | I07/I13/I17/I33/I39/I40 | Same snapshot native comparison per basis input; definedness/classifier reporting; amplification and named-target estimate |
| 8 | Plain-C Monte Carlo amplitude estimation | I05/I06/I07/I13/I15/I17/I33 | Entropy as superposed input; deterministic FP/libm; oracle/simulation/estimate gates |

Every rung remains in scope at full programme scale. Minis are diagnostics for mechanisms; the §8 end-to-end programmes remain the conformance repository's consumer gates under NS§6.1. The runtime libraries, VM, specialiser and estimator remain separately replaceable implementations of published contracts; the outline proposes no shared code across a boundary. A huge conservative width construction or slow replay is an efficiency concern, not authority to reject promised programs.

## Part F. Numbered maintainer decisions

The following are proposed questions, not rulings. Each item record names exactly one decision; D01 additionally gates every0.9 adoption. Recommendations retain all capabilities even if mechanics are staged.

**D01. Approve separate compatible 0.2.11/ISA 0.8 and capability 0.3/ISA 0.9 tracks, with publication and execution gates rather than immediate retargeting?**

Recommendation: Yes. Preserve accepted bytes; gate 0.3 on published ISA 0.9, muse disposition, maintainer K64/K65 ratification, published reference-library binding and independent ISA 0.9 execution. K64/K65 define non-row support and whole-cleanup ordinals (ISA-11-P10, ISA-11-P11), relevant to all scheduled/certificate realisations, not new LLVM capability exclusions.

**D02. Retain the staged per-region table and all four budget units, with no new table ABI?**

Recommendation: Yes; I05/I33 retain canonical identity selection, counters and sstack accounting.

**D03. Retain the version2 descriptor with independent ARG/IN/MEM_IN/OUT bindings and existing common-delivery scheme?**

Recommendation: Yes; I06/I17, including zero-output execution and 2k-1 wide delivery.

**D04. Retain the full lifted closure and all seven own-format FP implementations?**

Recommendation: Yes; I07/I15 keep the complete generic-intrinsic inventory, vector/limb order and native-materialised differential contract.

**D05. Retain the four per-tier device models and libc/VFS shims as ordinary bitcode?**

Recommendation: Yes; I13, with R terminal faults updated by D18 and no silent host execution in R/L.

**D06. Retain polyvariant clones, one A(F), shared globals and bounded-program compare-tree dispatch?**

Recommendation: Yes; I22/I35/I23/I30. No new indirect-call ISA opcode is required.

**D07. Retain the exact field/interval-sensitive reference analysis and general allocator/call-return propagation?**

Recommendation: Yes; I11/I24/I26/I27/I28, with no reliance on discarded range metadata or declared array size as an index bound.

**D08. Retain Cl/M/Co TDL and direct-body handling of reversible markers during action generation?**

Recommendation: Yes; I01/I31, including clean control workspace and accepted overwrite/inverse obligations.

**D09. Retain requested deferred measurement and the accepted benign-attribute lists?**

Recommendation: Yes; I08/I21, with channel-boundary and LLVM-legality evidence kept distinct.

**D10. Retain runtime op/class dispatch over the complete valid tables?**

Recommendation: Yes; I04/I18. Out-of-table cases retain their existing precise diagnostics.

**D11. Retain clean fallback for CC-spanning borrowing and allocation-generation handle equality?**

Recommendation: Yes; I09/I10. Direct dirty borrowing outside actions remains optional efficiency, not an extra blocker.

**D12. Retain EH/sjlj/fibers, target asm classification, varargs and the complete riscv64 platform?**

Recommendation: Yes; I12/I36/I37/I39. Continue platform inventory and actual boot/oracle gates; no unsupported-subset shortcut.

**D13. Retain tested mixed-major linkage and closed-unit mode with whole-program fallback?**

Recommendation: Yes; I16/I34. Actual link execution remains required evidence.

**D14. Choose native K4 only for covering exhaustive/library claims, while retaining CC expansion for the existing sampled/assumed inverse path?**

Recommendation: Choose that dual construction; I03/I14. Do not reinterpret sampled evidence or T-CLEAN as a native K4 proof.

**D15. Adopt dynamic classical qreg widths and coherent padding to a sound materialised/type-domain bound?**

Recommendation: Yes; I19. Keep n>=1 and transfer checks. Full unsigned i64-domain padding is the conservative fallback when AV top supplies no smaller bound; cost can be enormous but the analysis cap is not a scope cap.

**D16. Adopt exact device budget.mint with tuple-bound schedules and reversible token validity?**

Recommendation: Yes; I20. Preserve existing host/static paths and forbid tuple rounding.

**D17. Adopt persistent lossless tag-to-ledger-id lookup across entries, with exclusive run ownership?**

Recommendation: Yes; I25/I29/I-cbe. Keep pointer-width tags and an explicit bijection/bridge rather than assuming every pointer can store an ISA64 id; remove static-file bounds only on the0.9 target.

**D18. Adopt K2/K5/K6 R ledger operations, typed terminal faults and eligible failed-run reversal?**

Recommendation: Yes; I02/I32/I38/I-bz6. R faults terminate the whole run, not return status to a continuing CC caller; budget enforcement stays dialect instrumentation.

**D19. Retain runtime entry replay and extend its snapshot preparation/release to R using K2?**

Recommendation: Yes; I40. No overlay primitive or build-time-only restriction; charge replay and preserve allocator identity.

**D20. Treat 0t9 as an already-applied example correction, pending regression evidence rather than another spec change?**

Recommendation: Yes; I-0t9. Keep candidates=classical in the inherited example and test first-operand order.

**D21. Clarify PT12 using the complete virtual N9 value transfer in the compatible track?**

Recommendation: Yes; I-0y2. Execute xor and both cmpxchg paths; preserve its nonblocking status relative to accepted 0.2.10.

**D22. Adopt ISA 0.9 invocation/claim binding while retaining metadata fallbacks and honest fixed-region evidence labels?**

Recommendation: Yes; I-gwf. No schema invention and no claim of full-D proof for a strict fixed-image subdomain; rung4 verification remains complete on its declared fixed-database space.

## ISA proposal disposition

**New ISA beads filed: none.** K1/K2/K3/K5/K6 contain the actual missing0.8 capability sentences. K4 is optional and does not replace the sampled CC construction. G-12/13/14/15/16, direct CC dirty borrowing and native indirect calls retain explicit constructions; they are efficiency work, not newly discovered ISA blockers. Exact sentences formerly needed for bz6/cbe are cited in those records. Fixed-region proof labelling and dedicated certificate metadata fields remain wording/schema questions in the already-open gwf bead, with capability-preserving metadata/evidence defaults. K64/K65 need ratification, not duplicate proposal beads.

# 2cg — distinguish exact primitive history from a minimal C-wrapper fixture

Council proposal/disposition for `b-dialect-spec-2cg` (dialect repository).

## Defect and North Star scope

Both maintained dialect texts, §17.1 “C allocation alignment”, require
ISA §7.1's exact primitive bump, requested-size record and nonreclaiming
history. C payload extents are ABI-rounded; alignment padding is explicit
and minimal. Neither clause publishes a runtime query for old requested
sizes/liveness. The bead asks whether the exact three-record
`scripts/0212_allocation.c` witness also limits every production allocator.

The actual fixture calls itself an **independent primitive/wrapper trace
fixture, not a production allocator**. Runtime ordinary-bitcode bodies
are materialised on both sides (dialect §§17.7/19.1). On that reading,
ordinary charged bookkeeping is already expressible. This is a genuine
wording/conformance-boundary ambiguity worth resolving, **not evidence
that a new ISA allocation-query instruction is necessary**, and not a
remaining unimplemented realloc in the originating runtime. Its later
source/request supersedes the earlier blocked-query interpretation.

North Star §§3.2/5/6.1 and §8 rungs 4–5 requires real libc, unmodified
SQLite and allocator-based Julia collections. A fixed record cap,
native-only allocation query, copying past the old payload or dropping
realloc would narrow scope. Ordinary dynamic metadata preserves it;
actual added budget/history costs must remain visible and reversible.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Origin read: runtimes `.tmp/sol-runtimes-queue/cross-repo-beads.md`,
“resolve realloc size/liveness bookkeeping”, its implementation update,
and “wider allocator re-pin”; `src/allocator/bump.c` was inspected
read-only. It keeps requested sizes/live records in explicit 32-byte
heap records, reuses freed metadata slots as data, and uses an explicit
zero-size primitive probe before alignment. It does not reclaim payload
addresses or access the ISA's private allocation table.

With heap base 4096, alignment 16 and an initial raw `alloc(3)`:

| Program/body | Subsequent primitive requests | Payload base | Final bump | Records including raw alloc |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Minimal fixture `cmalloc(1)` | 13 padding, 16 payload | 4112 | 4128 | 3 |
| Inspected production `malloc(1)`, empty metadata pool | 0 probe, 12 padding, 16 payload, 32 metadata | 4112 | 4160 | 5 |

The zero-size primitive advances one byte but records requested size 0
(ISA §7.1). The production body therefore needs 64 heap bytes in this
fresh case, rather than 32. With a 32-byte heap its metadata allocation
faults after the successful probe/padding/payload prefix; ordinary
function calls do not imply transaction-wide rollback. Reversal restores
the prefix's records and stores by the ordinary reversible history.

The old-size information witness is also valid: allocations 16 then 32,
or 32 then 16, have the same first pointer p and final bump p+48. A
cursor alone cannot tell whether `realloc(p,48)` preserves 16 or 32
bytes. This proves a need to retain sizes, not a need for a privileged
query: a side list written at each allocation supplies the information.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Treat the minimal fixture as the only allowed production trace | Requires another published metadata mechanism to retain full libc scope. |
| Publish a new allocation-size/liveness primitive | Possible larger contract change; unnecessary for the inspected implementation. |
| Permit ordinary explicit charged bookkeeping | Keeps exact primitive semantics and native/device equality. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

In **both** maintained maps append to §17.1 after the allocation vector:

```text
The allocation vector fixes the trace of its stated thin wrapper,
not a universal trace for every implementation of malloc/calloc/realloc.
Runtime bodies may retain requested size, liveness and alignment state
in ordinary materialised memory, including dynamically allocated metadata
and explicit zero-size address probes. Every such operation, its stores,
padding and allocation history is present identically in device and
native materialisation and charged to the actual heap/step/table budgets.
The C payload request retains the rounded extent and returned-base rule
above; padding immediately before that payload is minimal from the
actual current primitive bump. No hidden header, private ISA-table query,
native-only map or fixed metadata cap is implied or permitted by this
allowance. Reusing live metadata storage as ordinary data does not reclaim
primitive payload addresses or allocation-table/history entries.

realloc preserves min(old requested size,new size) bytes of a live C
allocation, using retained ordinary metadata or another published
mechanism. Invalid/dead/interior pointers retain the materialised
memory/fault contract. Overflow preflights precede the corresponding
allocation mutation; a later primitive failure retains the successfully
executed ordinary prefix unless explicitly reversed. Both comparison
builds execute the same body, including its failure prefix. Tests of a
production runtime bind that body's identity and exact resulting trace;
they do not impose the thin fixture's record count on a different body.
```

Keep the three-record fixture and its output exactly as published. Add a
separate tagged production-body vector to the dialect/runtime acceptance
corpus, not a replacement that weakens primitive history checks. This
proposal grants no blanket memory-check exemption to runtime functions.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

ISA primitive semantics and ref catalog bytes stay fixed. Different
materialised runtime bodies change bitcode/native manifests, dialect
materialisation identity, generated ISA closures, resource costs and
consumer pins; exact addresses may change too. A dialect wording revision
must obey its full-version/mapping policy. Driver/plugin/conformance must
re-pin and budget the actual body, not transplant minimal fixture costs.
No sibling publication or tracker database is written here.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Check both traces above, min(16,48)=16 versus min(32,48)=32, shrink,
null/zero-size, dead/interior pointer, direct intervening primitive alloc,
pool-slot reuse, first stronger alignment and failures at each primitive.
Reverse all actual metadata/list stores and primitive records and replay
identically; never test only the returned payload. Exact hand arithmetic
is checked in [INDEX](INDEX.md). The runtime's reported LLVM18/23 gates
were read as originating evidence, not rerun here; this job runs no
full sibling gate and claims no SQLite/Julia rung acceptance.

## Release and route

Recommend a **current dialect §17.1 clarification**, in the 0.2 and 0.3
maps under their publication rules; no ISA 0.9 K or ISA 0.10 dependency.
Retain related jjr/0na and re-pin work with their owners as appropriate.
The orchestrator can attach this note to 2cg; no new cross-repo bead is
needed because the decision is already tracked there.

## Publication and combined schema decisions

“0.9 K repair” means the existing council-reviewed repair track, not
permission to alter a published semantic contract silently. `spec/README.md`
normally requires a full-version bump for semantic changes. Council must
choose that publication route and explicit subformat/ABI compatibility
rules. Every note's unchanged-source-hash statement assumes an authorized
in-version repair with unchanged source envelope bytes. A version bump
changes all affected version-prefixed envelopes and consumer pins. The
ref catalog changes only if its own versioned bytes change; none of these
proposals needs a silent rewrite of `ref@0.9`.

Use one context vocabulary across v2v/cwq/jw7: `context_key` is exactly
§12.1's concrete invocation chain in the bound root realisation, including
instance, call_index, loop iterations and applicable oracle step/phase.
The root key is []; nested default-covered calls are included. Resolve
variant/controls/arguments/budget/placements from that tree. Order arrays
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of each key's JCS encoding. Frame/resource/
fault arrays validate the same context identities where applicable;
variant/control-count alone is never an invocation identifier. K12 exact
rationals and outward resource display remain unchanged. ysw separately
adds the missing token equality relation; it does not change params_hash
or serialize process ids.

Recommended acceptance groups are applicability/owner binding (0jq),
frames and reports (4ta/v2v/cwq/jw7), borrow proofs (7s0), token context
(ysw), and native models (95w). The fixture and migration dispositions
should proceed with their existing owners. K numbers are assigned only
at acceptance, avoiding collisions with concurrent council work.

## Reproducible finite checks

From the repository root under the environment prefix:

```sh
timeout 300 python3 -I -B -u - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('docs/notes/2026-11-02-pass2/INDEX.md')

PY
```

This code checks exact small mathematical witnesses and actual published
reference interfaces. The arithmetic is not an implementation of any new
certificate verifier or a substitute for its required conformance tests.

```python
from pathlib import Path
from itertools import product
from fractions import Fraction as F
import copy
import hashlib
import json
import os
import re
import subprocess

ROOT = Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
OUT = ROOT / 'docs/notes/2026-11-02-pass2'
SCRATCH = ROOT / '.tmp/sol-contract-pass2'
REF = SCRATCH / 'target/debug/bennett-isa-ref'
PROBE = SCRATCH / 'target/debug/k82-probe'
assert Path.cwd() == ROOT
assert os.environ['TMPDIR'] == str(SCRATCH / 'tmp')
assert os.environ['CARGO_TARGET_DIR'] == str(SCRATCH / 'target')
assert not any(k in os.environ for k in
               ('DISPLAY', 'WAYLAND_DISPLAY', 'DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS', 'BROWSER'))

isa = ['0jq', '4ta', 'v2v', '7s0', '95w', '0n5', 'jw7', 'cwq',
       'ysw', '552', 'ojy']
ids = ['b-isa-spec-' + x for x in isa] + [
       'b-dialect-spec-2cg', 'b-dialect-spec-djm']
assert {p.name for p in OUT.iterdir()} == {x + '.md' for x in ids} | {'INDEX.md'}
for ident in ids:
    p = OUT / (ident + '.md')
    s = p.read_text()
    for title in ('Defect', 'North Star', 'Alternatives',
                  'Recommended exact spec diff', 'Hashes', 'Conformance', 'Release'):
        assert title.lower() in s.lower(), (ident, title)
    assert ident in s and not re.search(r'\b(?:TODO|TBD)\b', s)
for p in OUT.glob('*.md'):
    s = p.read_text()
    assert sum(line.startswith('```') for line in s.splitlines()) % 2 == 0, p
    # Remove fenced examples before treating Markdown syntax as links.
    prose = re.sub(r'```[^\n]*\n.*?\n```', '', s, flags=re.S)
    for link in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]+\]\(([^)]+)\)', prose):
        if not link.startswith(('http://', 'https://', '#')):
            assert (p.parent / link.split('#')[0]).exists(), (p, link)
index = (OUT / 'INDEX.md').read_text()
for ident in ids:
    assert index.count('| [' + ident + ']') == 1, ident
print('PASS: 13 per-bead notes and index; required sections, fences and links')

# 0jq: H = M/sqrt(2), so M*M=2I exactly; each H column has two nonzeros.
M = ((1, 1), (1, -1))
assert [[sum(M[i][k]*M[k][j] for k in range(2)) for j in range(2)]
        for i in range(2)] == [[2, 0], [0, 2]]
assert all(sum(M[i][j] != 0 for i in range(2)) == 2 for j in range(2))
# 95w: Bell +/- have norm squared 2 before division by sqrt(2), overlap zero.
plus, minus = [1, 0, 0, 1], [1, 0, 0, -1]
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip(plus, minus)) == 0
assert sum(a*a for a in plus) == sum(a*a for a in minus) == 2
assert F(1)-F(1) == 0

# 4ta, v2v, cwq: exact basis maps and separate root/child footprints.
for t in range(4):
    assert (t ^ 3) ^ 3 == t
for t, u in product(range(2), repeat=2):
    assert ((t ^ 1) ^ 1, (u ^ 1) ^ 1) == (t, u)
assert (2, 1*2, 1*2, 1, 1) == (2, 2, 2, 1, 1)  # split time, A/B occupancy/peak
child_footprints = [(1, 1, 1), (1, 2, 2)]  # time, occupancy, peak
root = (2, 2*2, 2)
assert root == (2, 4, 2)
assert sum(c[2] for c in child_footprints) != root[2]
assert root[1] + sum(c[1] for c in child_footprints) != root[1]
assert F(1) + F(1,4) == F(5,4) and F(2) + F(1,4) == F(9,4)

# 7s0: a finite model of K76's all-operand stack rule for these CX-only prefixes.
def entry_c(sequence):
    values = {'a': None, 'x': None, 'c': 0}
    stacks = {w: [] for w in values}
    events = []
    for control, target in sequence:  # cx and mcx(1) already normalized to one kind
        identity = (control, target)
        operands = (control, target)
        top = stacks[control][-1] if stacks[control] else None
        if (top is not None and all(stacks[w] and stacks[w][-1] == top
                                   for w in operands)
                and events[top][0] == identity):
            before = events[top][1]
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].pop()
                values[w] = before[w]
        elif values[control] == 0:
            continue  # disabled: no causal event
        else:
            event = len(events)
            events.append((identity, {w: values[w] for w in operands}))
            for w in operands:
                stacks[w].append(event)
            values[target] = (values[target] ^ values[control]
                              if all(values[w] is not None for w in operands)
                              else None)
    return values['c']
assert entry_c([]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('a','c')]) == 0
assert entry_c([('a','c'), ('x','c'), ('a','c'), ('x','c')]) is None
def frame_ok(c_values):
    return all((c, b ^ c) == (c, b) for c in c_values for b in range(2))
assert frame_ok([0]) and not frame_ok([0, 1])
assert not frame_ok(sorted(set([0] + [1])))  # repeated-visit D_F union cannot be narrowed
entry_products = [(0,0), (1,1)]
assert all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d in entry_products for b in range(2))
assert not all(b ^ c ^ d == b for c,d,b in product(range(2), repeat=3))
assert sum(a*b for a, b in zip((1,1), (1,-1))) == 0  # phase on B changes |+>

# 0n5: legal state size versus sample count, without allocating it.
assert (1 << 63) // 8 == (1 << 60)
# jw7: all input/target/fault tuples; F_B's code, not final XOR word.
for budget, expected_faults in [(1, 256), (2, 0)]:
    faults = 0
    for x, target, fault in product(range(4), range(4), range(16)):
        payload, code = (0, 1) if budget == 1 else ((x+1) % 4, 0)
        after = (x, target ^ payload, fault ^ code)
        assert 0 <= after[1] < 4 and 0 <= after[2] < 16
        faults += code != 0
    assert faults == expected_faults
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0 and 1 ^ 0 == 1

# ysw: identity partitions are independent of canonical tuple values.
def partition(handles):
    seen, result = {}, []
    for h in handles:
        if h not in seen:
            seen[h] = len(seen)
        result.append(seen[h])
    return result
def valid_labels(labels):
    return (all(type(x) is int and x >= 0 for x in labels)
            and labels == partition(labels))
assert partition([10,20,20]) == [0,1,1]
assert partition([10,20,30]) == [0,1,2]
assert partition([20,20]) == [0,0] and partition([]) == []
assert all(not valid_labels(x) for x in ([1,1], [0,2], [0,-1]))
args = [['1','0','0','0'], ['1','0','0','0']]
def jcs(x):
    return json.dumps(x, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':'), ensure_ascii=False).encode()
params = hashlib.sha256(jcs(args)).hexdigest()
assert params == hashlib.sha256(jcs(copy.deepcopy(args))).hexdigest()
assert jcs({'args':args, 'token_aliases':[0,0]}) != jcs(
           {'args':args, 'token_aliases':[0,1]})
assert (4 if partition([20,20]) == [0,0] else 3) == 4
assert (4 if partition([20,30]) == [0,0] else 3) == 3

# 2cg: exact primitive request/bump history; production metadata stores are not simulated.
def allocation_trace(production, limit):
    bump, records = 4096, []
    def alloc(n):
        nonlocal bump
        if bump + max(n, 1) > 4096 + limit:
            raise OverflowError
        p = bump
        records.append((p, n))
        bump += max(n, 1)
        return p
    payload, code = None, 0
    try:
        alloc(3)
        if production:
            alloc(0)
        padding = (-bump) % 16
        if padding:
            alloc(padding)
        payload = alloc(16)
        if production:
            alloc(32)
    except OverflowError:
        code = 2
    return payload, bump, records, code
minimal = allocation_trace(False, 32)
production = allocation_trace(True, 64)
assert minimal == (4112, 4128, [(4096,3),(4099,13),(4112,16)], 0)
assert production == (4112, 4160,
                      [(4096,3),(4099,0),(4100,12),(4112,16),(4128,32)], 0)
failed = allocation_trace(True, 32)
assert failed == (4112, 4128, production[2][:-1], 2)
assert 16+32 == 32+16 == 48 and min(16,48) != min(32,48)
# djm: required public name/environment examples, not a musl process simulation.
assert '/bin/demo'.rsplit('/',1)[-1] == 'demo'
assert '/bin/'.rsplit('/',1)[-1] == ''
assert dict(s.split('=',1) for s in ['K=V'])['K'] == 'V'
print('PASS: structural, frame/resource, borrow, census, token, allocation and process witnesses')

def run(argv):
    p = subprocess.run([str(x) for x in argv], cwd=ROOT, text=True,
                       capture_output=True, timeout=120)
    assert p.returncode == 0, (argv, p.returncode, p.stdout, p.stderr)
    return p.stdout

# ojy: independent written-order sweep of the current 54 bare-formal recipes.
catalog_path = ROOT / 'spec/ref-0.9.json'
raw = catalog_path.read_bytes()
recipes = {r['kind']:r for r in json.loads(raw)['recipes']}
pin_ref = '5de4e4995350313e50217880ba1b95de39cd043283b726337d4894efb005d197'
assert hashlib.sha256(b'isa=0.9\nexpansion=ref\nversion=0.9\n'+raw+b'\n').hexdigest() == pin_ref
body_interfaces = {}
for kind, recipe in recipes.items():
    if not all(isinstance(p,str) for p in recipe['parameters']):
        continue
    used = []
    def walk(body, bound):
        for node in body:
            if 'gate' in node:
                for wire in node['wires']:
                    name = wire.split('[',1)[0]
                    if name not in bound and name not in used:
                        used.append(name)
            elif 'alloc' in node:
                bound.add(node['alloc'])
            elif 'for' in node:
                walk(node['body'], bound | {node['for']})
            elif 'inverse' in node:
                walk(node['body'], bound)
            elif 'release' in node:
                assert node['release'] in bound
            else:
                raise AssertionError((kind, node))  # no unexamined constructors
    walk(recipe['body'], set(recipe['parameters']))
    body_interfaces[kind] = used
assert len(body_interfaces) == 54
changed = [k for k,v in body_interfaces.items() if v != sorted(v)]
assert len(changed) == 31
assert body_interfaces['value.select'] == ['p','b','a','t']

# Execute the existing generator only through document construction, before its file-write branch.
# The one-line correction is in memory; the published generator/fixture are never written.

scope = {'__file__':str(generator), '__name__':'pass2_in_memory_generator'}
exec(compile(source.replace('return sorted(used)', 'return used'), str(generator), 'exec'), scope)
corrected = scope['doc']
original_path = generator.parent / 'k82.json'
original = json.loads(original_path.read_text())
assert corrected['interfaces'] == body_interfaces
assert sum(original['interfaces'][k] != corrected['interfaces'][k]
           for k in body_interfaces) == 31
changed_edges = sum(a['interface'] != b['interface']
                    for a,b in zip(original['edges'], corrected['edges']))
assert len(original['edges']) == len(corrected['edges']) == 20
assert changed_edges == 10
without_enumeration = copy.deepcopy(corrected)
without_enumeration['interfaces'] = original['interfaces']
for old, new in zip(original['edges'], without_enumeration['edges']):
    new['interface'] = old['interface']
assert without_enumeration == original
assert sum(len(e['instances']) for e in corrected['edges']) == 86
assert len(corrected['cases']) == 16
corrected_path = SCRATCH / 'k82-body-order.json'
corrected_path.write_bytes(jcs(corrected) + b'\n')
for path in (original_path, corrected_path):
    output = run([PROBE, path, catalog_path])
    assert 'edges: 86 match, 0 differ' in output
    assert sum(': PASS' in line for line in output.splitlines()) == 16, output
print('PASS: K82 31/54 ordering mismatches;', changed_edges,
      'edge interface arrays differ; all other fields identical; both probes 86/86 and 16/16')
print('K82 original fixture SHA-256:', hashlib.sha256(original_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest())
print('K82 corrected scratch fixture SHA-256:', hashlib.sha256(corrected_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest())
print('PASS: pass2 verification complete; proposed schemas remain unimplemented/unaccepted')

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:1028:| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4769:| N4 | attributes on device function definitions, their parameters and returns, and on every call site in a device function: `noundef`, `nonnull`, `range(...)`, `align` (except together with `byval`, `byref`, `sret`, `inalloca`, `preallocated`), `dereferenceable(...)`, `dereferenceable_or_null(...)`, `nofpclass(...)`, `noalias`, `nocapture`, `captures(...)`, parameter `readnone`/`readonly`/`writeonly`, `returned`, `writable`, `dead_on_unwind`, `initializes(...)`, `allocalign`, `allocptr`; function attributes `memory(...)`, `readnone`, `readonly`, `writeonly`, `argmemonly`, `inaccessiblememonly`, `inaccessiblemem_or_argmemonly`, `willreturn`, `mustprogress`, `nosync`, `nofree`, `norecurse`, `speculatable`, `personality` (after N28) | removed (attribute groups are rewritten; a group shared with an H function is first duplicated) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4798:| N25 | `byval`, `inalloca` and `preallocated` arguments; `llvm.call.preallocated.*`; aggregate returns wider than 128 bits | an explicit caller copy into a shadow-stack slot of the caller's frame (`%nz.bv<j>.<r>` for argument j) passed as a plain `ptr`, and the attribute removed from the callee and the call; a return of a first-class aggregate wider than 128 bits is demoted to a hidden first parameter `ptr %nz.ret` in which the callee stores the value (`sret` semantics) |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4842:**`n-defined-zero`.** N12 totalises the flags before N27. This does not exempt abs, ctlz or cttz from expansion: full materialised output contains no calls to these LLVM intrinsics, including false-flag calls. At x=0 both counts are 8, so the sum is 16; at x=1 they are 7 and 0, so the sum is 7.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:4878:materialised output, since source poison/freeze does not promise these values.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:8446:**`binding-clock-read`.** CLOCK(3) denotes initial c=3. This materialised clock read returns the old counter and advances its private copy to 4. Every invocation starts at 3. Four prologue rows, one call, ten callee rows and one return total 16; the store materialises its global-address operand anew.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:8568:**`binding-console-byte`.** CONSOLE(1) denotes L=1, with a non-null one-byte output register and null return target. The materialised one-byte write appends x (120), advancing n from 0 to 1; delivery XORs 0x78 into the output without a host effect. Twelve prologue rows, call, eleven callee rows and three delivery rows total 27.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10003:## 17. Device classical code: memory model, lowering, faults
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10005:### 17.1 Memory model
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10026:**C allocation alignment.** The `__bennett_nz_alloc(n)` primitive MUST retain ISA §7.1's exact byte bump by max(n,1), zero fill, requested-size allocation record and nonreclaiming history; neither backend may insert hidden alignment into that primitive.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10028:The C `malloc`/`calloc` wrappers MUST provide the target ABI's `max_align_t` alignment by requesting a rounded extent from that primitive: with a = ABI alignment of `max_align_t`, allocate a × ceil(max(n,1)/a) bytes, checking multiplication/rounding overflow as fault 2 before mutation; `free` passes the returned base, and both builds use the same wrapper and history.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10032:The §16.9 thin wrapper is a specialised witness for its only allocation size, 16 bytes (already aligned on its target), not a replacement for C allocation alignment after arbitrary odd-sized requests. Padding belongs to the wrapper's live allocation and budget charge; it does not change the primitive. Aligned allocation with a stronger requested alignment remains a runtimes operation, not a new primitive.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10210:materialised device variants, code-generated by the same LLVM major and linked with
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10213:the materialised optimised module is the one program both builds implement. The driver MUST make

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10218:**Comparison-only support exception.** Only after the materialised device module has been fixed, the native comparison backend MAY define the three `__bennett_nz_` primitives of §3.1 as native support, and the driver MUST emit `__bennett_rt_native_layout` as specified in §19.5; these definitions MUST NOT enter producer inputs, production device bitcode, region identity or the materialised module used by the ISA map.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10220:The native primitive support MUST reproduce the resolved device addresses, zero fill, live allocation sizes, nonreclaiming allocation/free history and fault outcomes of §§17.1/17.5, with capacity for every history admitted by the resolved heap budget (at most one heap record per byte since max(n,1) ≥ 1), without an arbitrary smaller record cap; host setup/resource failure MUST terminate comparison with an explicit infrastructure failure, never silently relocate the heap, choose defaults, use host malloc, or report a successful dialect outcome.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md:10222:**Definedness classifiers.** The comparison binary MUST detect the materialised memory faults of §17.5 (out-of-live-allocation access, use after free and invalid/double free), division by zero and signed division overflow, and reached explicit fault/unreachable paths; instrumentation or a checked memory adapter may supply these checks outside the materialised module, and UBSan-only coverage MUST NOT be reported as satisfying the memory-fault obligation.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1005:### 5.2 Rules
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1016:not permit omitting a value produced by the materialised execution.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1024:| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1038:**PT12 N9 update witnesses (DB/DN/DS; non-normative).** The global starts at 1. Xor by 2 alternates 1 and 3 in the flow-insensitive least fixpoint; a single execution allocates width 3. Cmpxchg against 1 first succeeds and writes 3; subsequent abstract iterations include failure on old 3. Against 9 it always fails and leaves 1. Native LLVM 18/23 materialised O0/O3 checks old/success, actual width, clean free, liveness and code 0.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1108:**PT12 spelling witnesses (non-normative).** Explicit address spaces, volatile/weak qualifiers, syncscope and alignment retain the same N9 value effects and native materialised outcomes; the pointer operand keeps its address-space type in the generated load and store.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1180:All accept. LLVM 18/23 verify source O0; N24/N8/N15/N21 materialisation and
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:1183:the comparison executes the specified zero-filled materialised program.

spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:10018:**C allocation alignment.** The `__bennett_nz_alloc(n)` primitive MUST retain ISA §7.1's exact byte bump by max(n,1), zero fill, requested-size allocation record and nonreclaiming history; neither backend may insert hidden alignment into that primitive.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:10020:The C `malloc`/`calloc` wrappers MUST provide the target ABI's `max_align_t` alignment by requesting a rounded extent from that primitive: with a = ABI alignment of `max_align_t`, allocate a × ceil(max(n,1)/a) bytes, checking multiplication/rounding overflow as fault 2 before mutation; `free` passes the returned base, and both builds use the same wrapper and history.
spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md:10024:The §16.9 thin wrapper is a specialised witness for its only allocation size, 16 bytes (already aligned on its target), not a replacement for C allocation alignment after arbitrary odd-sized requests. Padding belongs to the wrapper's live allocation and budget charge; it does not change the primitive. Aligned allocation with a stronger requested alignment remains a runtimes operation, not a new primitive.

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

## Common Workflows

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work
bd show <id>       # Review issue details
bd update <id> --claim  # Claim it
```

**Completing work:**
```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once
git status                  # Check changed files
# Conservative/minimal/default: report status and proposed commands; wait for approval
# Team-maintainer opt-in only, unless current instructions forbid it:
# git add . && git commit -m "..."
# git push
```

**Creating dependent work:**
```bash
# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)
bd create --title="Implement feature X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=feature
bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

> **Note**: AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are independent files (not symlinked and not sharing an inode). Mirror substantive edits across both, or symlink one to the other.

/* Independent primitive/wrapper trace fixture, not a production allocator. */
#include <stdint.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stddef.h>
#include <limits.h>
struct record { uint64_t base, requested, old_bump; };
struct heap { unsigned char bytes[32]; uint64_t bump,count,limit; struct record records[32]; };
static unsigned alloc(struct heap *h,uint64_t n,uint64_t *p){
 uint64_t extent=n?n:1;
 if(extent>h->limit || h->bump>h->limit-extent || h->count==32)return 2;
 *p=4096+h->bump;
 h->records[h->count++]=(struct record){*p,n,h->bump};
 memset(h->bytes+h->bump,0,extent);h->bump+=extent;return 0;
}
static unsigned cmalloc(struct heap *h,uint64_t n,uint64_t *p){
 uint64_t size=n?n:1,unused;
 if(size>UINT64_MAX-15)return 2;
 uint64_t extent=(size+15)&~UINT64_C(15),pad=(-(4096+h->bump))&15;
 if(pad){unsigned code=alloc(h,pad,&unused);if(code)return code;}
 return alloc(h,extent,p);
}
/* Reverse the ordinary primitive allocation, restoring its pre-state bytes. */
static void reverse(struct heap *h){
 struct record r=h->records[--h->count];
 memset(h->bytes+r.old_bump,0xaa,h->bump-r.old_bump);h->bump=r.old_bump;
 memset(h->records+h->count,0,sizeof r);
}
int main(void){
 struct heap h={0};memset(h.bytes,0xaa,sizeof h.bytes);h.limit=32;
 struct heap initial=h;uint64_t p,q;unsigned a=alloc(&h,3,&p),b=cmalloc(&h,1,&q);
 printf("[%u,%u,%llu,%llu,%llu,%llu]\n",a,b,(unsigned long long)p,(unsigned long long)q,(unsigned long long)(4096+h.bump),(unsigned long long)h.count);
 for(unsigned k=0;k<h.count;k++)printf("[%llu,%llu]\n",(unsigned long long)h.records[k].base,(unsigned long long)h.records[k].requested);
 unsigned nonzero=0;for(unsigned k=0;k<32;k++)nonzero|=h.bytes[k];printf("%u\n",nonzero);
 struct heap saved=h;unsigned code=alloc(&h,1,&q);printf("[%u,%d]\n",code,!memcmp(&saved,&h,sizeof h));
 code=cmalloc(&h,UINT64_MAX,&q);printf("[%u,%d]\n",code,!memcmp(&saved,&h,sizeof h));
 while(h.count)reverse(&h);printf("%d\n",!memcmp(&initial,&h,sizeof h));
 h=initial;alloc(&h,3,&p);saved=h;code=cmalloc(&h,UINT64_MAX,&q);
 printf("[%u,%d]\n",code,!memcmp(&saved,&h,sizeof h));
 /* Padding fails without mutation; extent failure preserves successful padding. */
 for(unsigned limit=3;limit<=31;limit+=28){
  h=initial;h.limit=limit;alloc(&h,3,&p);saved=h;
  code=cmalloc(&h,1,&q);
  printf("[%u,%llu,%llu,%d]\n",code,(unsigned long long)h.bump,(unsigned long long)h.count,limit==3?!memcmp(&saved,&h,sizeof h):h.records[1].requested==13);
 }
 h=initial;alloc(&h,0,&p);printf("[%llu,%llu,%llu,%u]\n",(unsigned long long)p,(unsigned long long)h.bump,(unsigned long long)h.records[0].requested,h.bytes[0]);
}

# 2cg — distinguish exact primitive history from a minimal C-wrapper fixture

Council proposal/disposition for `b-dialect-spec-2cg` (dialect repository).

## Defect and North Star scope

Both maintained dialect texts, §17.1 “C allocation alignment”, require
ISA §7.1's exact primitive bump, requested-size record and nonreclaiming
history. C payload extents are ABI-rounded; alignment padding is explicit
and minimal. Neither clause publishes a runtime query for old requested
sizes/liveness. The bead asks whether the exact three-record
`scripts/0212_allocation.c` witness also limits every production allocator.

The actual fixture calls itself an **independent primitive/wrapper trace
fixture, not a production allocator**. Runtime ordinary-bitcode bodies
are materialised on both sides (dialect §§17.7/19.1). On that reading,
ordinary charged bookkeeping is already expressible. This is a genuine
wording/conformance-boundary ambiguity worth resolving, **not evidence
that a new ISA allocation-query instruction is necessary**, and not a
remaining unimplemented realloc in the originating runtime. Its later
source/request supersedes the earlier blocked-query interpretation.

North Star §§3.2/5/6.1 and §8 rungs 4–5 requires real libc, unmodified
SQLite and allocator-based Julia collections. A fixed record cap,
native-only allocation query, copying past the old payload or dropping
realloc would narrow scope. Ordinary dynamic metadata preserves it;
actual added budget/history costs must remain visible and reversible.

## Alternatives and worked witness

Origin read: runtimes `.tmp/sol-runtimes-queue/cross-repo-beads.md`,
“resolve realloc size/liveness bookkeeping”, its implementation update,
and “wider allocator re-pin”; `src/allocator/bump.c` was inspected
read-only. It keeps requested sizes/live records in explicit 32-byte
heap records, reuses freed metadata slots as data, and uses an explicit
zero-size primitive probe before alignment. It does not reclaim payload
addresses or access the ISA's private allocation table.

With heap base 4096, alignment 16 and an initial raw `alloc(3)`:

| Program/body | Subsequent primitive requests | Payload base | Final bump | Records including raw alloc |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Minimal fixture `cmalloc(1)` | 13 padding, 16 payload | 4112 | 4128 | 3 |
| Inspected production `malloc(1)`, empty metadata pool | 0 probe, 12 padding, 16 payload, 32 metadata | 4112 | 4160 | 5 |

The zero-size primitive advances one byte but records requested size 0
(ISA §7.1). The production body therefore needs 64 heap bytes in this
fresh case, rather than 32. With a 32-byte heap its metadata allocation
faults after the successful probe/padding/payload prefix; ordinary
function calls do not imply transaction-wide rollback. Reversal restores
the prefix's records and stores by the ordinary reversible history.

The old-size information witness is also valid: allocations 16 then 32,
or 32 then 16, have the same first pointer p and final bump p+48. A
cursor alone cannot tell whether `realloc(p,48)` preserves 16 or 32
bytes. This proves a need to retain sizes, not a need for a privileged
query: a side list written at each allocation supplies the information.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Treat the minimal fixture as the only allowed production trace | Requires another published metadata mechanism to retain full libc scope. |
| Publish a new allocation-size/liveness primitive | Possible larger contract change; unnecessary for the inspected implementation. |
| Permit ordinary explicit charged bookkeeping | Keeps exact primitive semantics and native/device equality. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

In **both** maintained maps append to §17.1 after the allocation vector:

```text
The allocation vector fixes the trace of its stated thin wrapper,
not a universal trace for every implementation of malloc/calloc/realloc.
Runtime bodies may retain requested size, liveness and alignment state
in ordinary materialised memory, including dynamically allocated metadata
and explicit zero-size address probes. Every such operation, its stores,
padding and allocation history is present identically in device and
native materialisation and charged to the actual heap/step/table budgets.
The C payload request retains the rounded extent and returned-base rule
above; padding immediately before that payload is minimal from the
actual current primitive bump. No hidden header, private ISA-table query,
native-only map or fixed metadata cap is implied or permitted by this
allowance. Reusing live metadata storage as ordinary data does not reclaim
primitive payload addresses or allocation-table/history entries.

realloc preserves min(old requested size,new size) bytes of a live C
allocation, using retained ordinary metadata or another published
mechanism. Invalid/dead/interior pointers retain the materialised
memory/fault contract. Overflow preflights precede the corresponding
allocation mutation; a later primitive failure retains the successfully
executed ordinary prefix unless explicitly reversed. Both comparison
builds execute the same body, including its failure prefix. Tests of a
production runtime bind that body's identity and exact resulting trace;
they do not impose the thin fixture's record count on a different body.
```

Keep the three-record fixture and its output exactly as published. Add a
separate tagged production-body vector to the dialect/runtime acceptance
corpus, not a replacement that weakens primitive history checks. This
proposal grants no blanket memory-check exemption to runtime functions.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

ISA primitive semantics and ref catalog bytes stay fixed. Different
materialised runtime bodies change bitcode/native manifests, dialect
materialisation identity, generated ISA closures, resource costs and
consumer pins; exact addresses may change too. A dialect wording revision
must obey its full-version/mapping policy. Driver/plugin/conformance must
re-pin and budget the actual body, not transplant minimal fixture costs.
No sibling publication or tracker database is written here.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Check both traces above, min(16,48)=16 versus min(32,48)=32, shrink,
null/zero-size, dead/interior pointer, direct intervening primitive alloc,
pool-slot reuse, first stronger alignment and failures at each primitive.
Reverse all actual metadata/list stores and primitive records and replay
identically; never test only the returned payload. Exact hand arithmetic
is checked in [INDEX](INDEX.md). The runtime's reported LLVM18/23 gates
were read as originating evidence, not rerun here; this job runs no
full sibling gate and claims no SQLite/Julia rung acceptance.

## Release and route

Recommend a **current dialect §17.1 clarification**, in the 0.2 and 0.3
maps under their publication rules; no ISA 0.9 K or ISA 0.10 dependency.
Retain related jjr/0na and re-pin work with their owners as appropriate.
The orchestrator can attach this note to 2cg; no new cross-repo bead is
needed because the decision is already tracked there.
